checker.js

every diagnostic, as check(source) — imported by fixer.js from beside it

Fetch the file itself if you can — https://www.appwithai.org/guide/checker.js — and ignore this page. It exists for one case: a fetch layer that reads pages but refuses application/javascript, and so reports the module as inaccessible while the site is answering normally.

bytes 258606
sha256 facf4823bab4b6d5e9fb72ac9c1bfb5e63746367b3cffae447a91ea7cdc254f4

The block below is that file, base64-encoded. Base64 carries no HTML-special character and ignores whitespace, so reflowing, wrapping or converting this page to Markdown cannot corrupt it. Reconstruct and verify:

base64 -d < checker.js.b64 > checker.js
sha256sum checker.js      # must print facf4823bab4b6d5e9fb72ac9c1bfb5e63746367b3cffae447a91ea7cdc254f4

Or in Node, with the block as a string:

writeFileSync("checker.js", Buffer.from(b64.replace(/\s+/g, ""), "base64"));

fixer.js imports checker.js from beside itself, so those two must land in the same directory. Then point a runner at it: node check-model.mjs <model>.mmd --base ./.
